S44a lands the Phase 2-A actor-identity and authorisation substrate per D126: ActorContext as a request-scoped shared-kernel value object, the `requires_authorisation` decorator at the use-case boundary, and `AuthorisationDenied` translated to 403. The session opened with a six-surface pre-write reconciliation that reshaped the framing-time D126 bundle before any code landed — the load-bearing event of the session, and the reason the brief's nine-commit plan closed at eight.

- Produced: Eight commits closed the session.
  - Commit 4254c43 (`docs(p13/s44a)`): D126; the p13-epic Session 2 → Session 2 (S44a) / Session 2b (S44b) split; the schema.md ActorContext sub-section under Cross-cutting binding shapes; the current-package transition; `briefs/p13/s44a.md` preserved with a pre-write reconciliation annotation recording the operator-confirmed reshape.
  - Commit b98c226 (`feat(p13/s44a)`): the ActorContext frozen dataclass at `shared_kernel/actor_context.py` — composes TenantContext plus `actor_id`, `role_list`, `authorisation_set`; 12 unit tests.
  - Commit fa5c9cf (`feat(p13/s44a)`): the `requires_authorisation` decorator, the `AuthorisationDenied` exception, and the Phase 2-A role-to-authorisation policy (five permission constants plus the `authorisations_for_roles` resolver) at `shared_kernel/authorisation.py`; the `AuthorisationDenied` → 403 handler at `apps/api/_auth_errors.py`; 23 tests.
  - Commit e1060de (`feat(p13/s44a)`): the `get_actor_context` FastAPI dependency at `apps/api/middleware.py`; 4 tests.
  - Commit 2b8539c (`feat(p13/s44a)`): the five portfolio use cases consuming ActorContext and applying the decorator; the CLI's ActorContext synthesis; use-case tests updated plus per-use-case deny-path tests.
  - Commit 8222813 (`feat(p13/s44a)`): the portfolio router wiring `get_actor_context`; router / HTTP-contract / tenant-isolation / integration test updates plus one new isolation scenario.
  - Commit 908c88a (`docs(p13/s44a)`): the six-stage live-stack smoke at `docs/smoke/p13_s44a_actor_context.md`; the compose.yaml padhanam-api digest pin advanced to the rebuilt image.
  - Commit this commit (`docs(p13/s44a)`): this session log entry; the current-package close marker; three captures entries; two `charter/phase-2-audit-inputs.md` close-hygiene entries.

- Decisions: D126 landed at commit 1, reshaped at pre-write reconciliation from the framing-time two-sub-commitment bundle to three sub-commitments — (1) the ActorContext value object, (2) the authorisation decorator plus `AuthorisationDenied`, (3) ActorReference retention as persisted authoring identity. D126 **supersedes D124's forward commitment** that `authored_by` and the Revisable Protocol's `actor` parameter become ActorContext — the first instance of a D-entry superseding a forward commitment carried by an earlier D-entry; D124 is not rewritten, the supersession is recorded in D126. Alternative (e) was added to the analysis (replace ActorReference per D124's literal commitment, rejected on the persisted-identity-versus-request-scoped-capability conflation). Kano: must-have.

- Tests: roughly 41 new tests across the shared_kernel, apps/api, and portfolio surfaces (ActorContext 12; authorisation decorator and policy 14; auth-error handler +4; get_actor_context +4; use-case deny-path +6; one new HTTP tenant-isolation scenario), plus signature-replacement updates to the portfolio router / HTTP-contract / integration suites. The full unit+contract+integration run is 1685 passed, 17 skipped. 28 of 28 import-linter contracts kept; AST enforcement green. Two pre-existing environmental failures (`contexts/methodology` lvt round-trip; `contexts/tools` `test_list_roles_using_tool_returns_empty_pre_commit_4`) fail in isolation against the live control-plane DB state — the `contexts/tools` test asserts a pre-migration-0012 empty binding set against a control plane that has 0012's eight seeded role-tool bindings applied. S44a touched zero files in either context's dependency closure; the failures are not S44a regressions.

- Enforcement layer: No new import-linter contracts. The two new shared_kernel modules (`actor_context.py`, `authorisation.py`) are policed by the existing `shared-kernel-policed` contract — both are framework-free (stdlib plus the sibling value objects only; no Pydantic, no `contexts/` imports), confirmed at 28/28. No AST enforcement change — S44a adds no bounded context or workspace package, so no Dockerfile COPY directive is required. The authorisation enforcement layer S44a adds is the `requires_authorisation` decorator at the use-case boundary: each of the five portfolio use cases carries a `@requires_authorisation(...)` line, and the decorator is the single grep-able surface a procurement auditor uses to verify completeness of authorisation enforcement across the codebase (the Decision 7 trivial-check-at-one-boundary commitment). Future use cases in P14+ inherit the decorator; the permission policy is single-sourced at `shared_kernel/authorisation.py`.

- Pre-write reconciliation: Six surfaces verified before commit 1; one was mechanical, five surfaced findings, and the findings reshaped D126 and the commit sequence. Surface 1 (D-number) confirmed D126 mechanically. Surface 2 (TenantContext consumption) verified the five use-case signatures. Surface 3 found no `apps/api/_auth.py` exists — TenantContext is built by the `get_tenant_context` dependency in `routers/inference.py`, not by middleware. Surface 4 found `tests/contract/revisable/` does not exist. Surface 5 — the central finding — found ActorReference consumed far beyond the brief's assumed "four use cases plus the Revisable Protocol": it is the declared type of `DataPoint.authored_by` and `Assertion.authored_by` (persisted domain-entity fields, round-tripped through a single `authored_by_user_id` text column) plus the adapter, the CLI, the DTO, the audit drafts, and five test files. Surface 6 (the new file topology budget surface) caught `apps/api/_errors.py` at 729 lines. All findings were surfaced to the operator as questions before commit 1; the operator confirmed five recommendations, reshaping D126 to retain ActorReference as the persisted authoring-identity value object and introduce ActorContext as new for request-scoped capability. The cumulative reconciliation count keeps climbing; S44a is the instance where reconciliation reshaped a charter decision rather than only absorbing drift.

- Reflection prompts answered:

  1. *Pre-write reconciliation surfaces.* The six surfaces split one mechanical (D-number) and five findings. Three were structural-honesty findings that became user-question resolutions: surface 5's ActorReference consumer surface (which falsified the brief's surface-5 assumption and forced the D126 reshape), surface 3's absent `_auth.py` (which re-homed the auth-middleware work to `middleware.py` plus `get_tenant_context`), and surface 6's 729-line `_errors.py`. Two were lighter — surface 4's absent contract-test directory (deferred) and surface 2 (confirmation plus the fifth-use-case discovery). The session is the clearest instance to date of pre-write reconciliation doing architectural work: it did not merely catch drift, it superseded a charter forward commitment (D124's). The discipline's value at S44a sits at the decision altitude, not the documentation-drift altitude.

  2. *Compose-shape decision validation.* The S44-framing Item 1 settlement on compose-shape (ActorContext wraps TenantContext as a field) held cleanly. Writing the code, the `tenant_context = actor.tenant_context` extraction appears once per use case — five sites — and reads as a deliberate boundary, not ceremony: the use case receives the rich request envelope and hands the adapter exactly the narrow value object it needs. The extraction is the structural boundary made visible, as Item 1 framing claimed. The stronger validation came at surface 5: compose-shape is *why* the reshape was clean. Because ActorContext composes rather than subsumes, ActorReference could stay exactly as it was for the persisted field while ActorContext took the request-scoped role — two single-concern value objects, no refactor of the domain entities, the adapter, or the DTO. A subsume-shape ActorContext would have forced the persisted-identity problem to be solved by either growing the schema or faking a reconstruction. First-instance evidence: compose-shape is structurally honest, and it bought the clean separation that resolved finding 1.

  3. *File topology budget first-instance evidence.* The budget did catch an overage, and it was load-bearing. Surface 6 found `apps/api/_errors.py` at 729 lines — far past a ~400-line ceiling for the router-error-handler file class — and the budget table's `_errors.py` row carried no current size, so absent the verification surface the overage would have gone unseen and the AuthorisationDenied handler would have landed there (the path the brief named), growing the file further. The discipline did two things at once: it redirected the handler to `apps/api/_auth_errors.py` (also the structurally correct home per D104's auth-cross-cutting placement) and surfaced the existing overage for forward triage. The discipline's load-bearing claim sits at first-instance validation, not only at recurrence. The `_errors.py` split is forwarded to the Phase 2-A close hygiene list; promotion of the discipline to the methodology document evaluates at P13 close per the framing settlement.

  4. *Framing-conversation pre-write reconciliation methodology observation.* The brief inherited D124's `authored_by`-becomes-ActorContext forward commitment without verifying it against the live domain model — the brief's surface-5 framing searched for ActorReference at the application-layer altitude only. The framing-conversation reconciliation candidate (framing conversations verify forward commitments against the live charter and codebase state before decomposing work) is reinforced here at a sharper edge: the thing that needed verifying was not charter prose but a *forward commitment's structural soundness against the persistence layer*. The mitigation surface is the same — read the live state before drafting the decomposition — but the lesson is that a D-entry's forward commitment is a hypothesis, not a settled instruction. Captured as a distinct methodology candidate (the forward-commitment-evaluation pattern; see methodology line 1).

  5. *Substrate-application boundary check.* The S41 substrate-application boundary candidate fired cleanly at S44a between commits 3 and 4. Commits 1-3 closed the shared_kernel additions (the value object, the decorator, the policy) — substrate that imports nothing from `contexts/` or `apps/`. Commits 4-6 opened the existing-code modifications (the middleware dependency, the five use cases, the router). The transition surfaced no scope drift: the substrate landed exactly as the reshaped D126 specified, and commits 4-6 were signature-and-wiring changes within the framing-time projection. The boundary is a genuine shape-change point — substrate is additive and self-contained; application work is modification with a blast radius — but at S44a it was a clean checkpoint, not a re-scoping moment. Recurrence holds the candidate at its existing threshold.

  6. *Methodology line opportunities.* Three candidates surfaced, each captured at `log/captures.md` and carried as a methodology line below. The compose-shape extraction idiom (`actor.tenant_context` at the adapter call seam) is a clean pattern but is the expected consequence of a settled framing decision, not structural novelty — not promoted, per the captures-candidate threshold. The authorisation-keys-must-match concern the brief flagged was dissolved rather than managed: the five permission strings are named once as constants in `shared_kernel/authorisation.py` and referenced by both the decorator applications and the lookup, so there is no parallel-drafting fragility to discipline.

- methodology (line 1): **Forward-commitment-evaluation pattern.** A forward commitment embedded in a D-entry is a hypothesis about future structure, not a binding instruction. D124 committed that ActorReference would be superseded by ActorContext at the `authored_by` field; S44a pre-write reconciliation evaluated that against the persistence layer, found it structurally unsound, and D126 supersedes it with reasoning. First instance of a D-entry superseding an earlier D-entry's forward commitment. Recurrence test: the next D-entry carrying a forward commitment evaluated at a future session. Captured at `log/captures.md`.

- methodology (line 2): **Brief-vs-domain-model drift at the type altitude.** A sub-class of the brief path-drift pattern (promoted at pre-S44 hygiene), but about which layers a type identifier reaches rather than file-location naming. The brief asserted ActorReference's consumer surface from the S44-framing mental model; the live surface spanned the domain entities, adapter, CLI, DTO, audit drafts, and tests. The same drift omitted the fifth use case (`create_data_point`). Discipline addition: a brief that names a type or a use-case set verifies it with an explicit identifier search across every layer. First-instance candidate; captured at `log/captures.md`.

- methodology (line 3): **File topology budget first-instance load-bearing evidence.** The discipline caught `apps/api/_errors.py` at 729 lines and redirected the AuthorisationDenied handler to its structurally-correct home, validating the discipline's load-bearing claim at first instance rather than at recurrence. Promotion to the methodology document evaluates at P13 close per the S44-framing settlement, with this evidence cited. Captured at `log/captures.md`.

- **S44a closed; the Phase 2-A identity-and-permissions substrate is in place end-to-end. The four S44-framing settlements held: compose-shape ActorContext was structurally honest and bought the clean reshape; the D126 bundle held with its body restructured to three sub-commitments; the file topology budget did load-bearing first-instance work; the D116 collapse (no architecture.md edit) was correct. What diverged was the brief's code-side assumptions about the live ActorReference surface — caught by reconciliation before any code landed. S44b lands the write-path substrate and transport per `briefs/p13/s44b.md`.**

## 2026-05-21 — Pre-S44 hygiene — brief path-drift methodology promotion
roles: technical writer, architect
mode: strategic (post-substrate-completion hygiene; promotes the methodology candidate queued at S43b close; no code changes)

- Produced: Two commits closed the session — one fewer than the brief's three-commit plan, because pre-write reconciliation found the captures-backfill commit unnecessary.
  - Commit e3f9457 (`docs(charter)`): the *Path-naming sub-pattern* paragraph under the pre-write reconciliation Patterns-observed entry at `charter/methodology.md`; the captures path-drift entry re-triaged as resolved at `log/captures.md`; the pre-S44 hygiene close marker at `charter/current-package.md`; `briefs/p13/pre-s44-hygiene.md` preserved at v1.
  - Commit this commit (`docs(p13/pre-s44-hygiene)`): this session log entry.

- Decisions: No new D-entries. Per hygiene-shape discipline — the promotion converts an already-surfaced methodology candidate to a methodology-document sub-paragraph; no architectural decision is made.

- Tests: None. Documentation-only changes. No edits under `contexts/`, `apps/`, `shared_kernel/`, `padhanam/`, `tests/`, or migrations.

- Pre-write reconciliation: Three surfaces verified before commit 1; all three surfaced findings, two of them material enough to surface to the operator before any commit landed.
  - Surface 1 (instance count): The S43b close framing carried three path-drift instances (S40, S43, S43b). Verbatim reading of each session's methodology lines found S40 records strategy-enumeration drift — D66 catalogues three retrieval strategies, the adapter executes two — not path naming. The "S40 adjacent adapter shape" citation in the S43 and S43b entries is a loose framing-time reference not borne out by S40's actual methodology lines. Verified count: two (S43, S43b).
  - Surface 2 (captures state): All three S43/S43b methodology candidates already exist at `log/captures.md`, landed at the S43b close commit. The captures-backfill commit the brief planned collapsed to empty per the brief's own conditional logic; only the path-drift entry's triage lines needed editing.
  - Surface 3 (methodology placement): The existing pre-write reconciliation entry carries the Mid-build sub-pattern paragraph as expected; the path-naming sub-paragraph landed adjacent to it per the brief's placement instruction.
  - A further finding outside the three named surfaces: the brief's own Entry B template and methodology sub-paragraph parenthetical inverted the S43 drift direction — they framed the S43 brief as naming `adapters/outbound/postgres/` against a flat convention, when the S43 session-log entry and the live codebase both show the reverse. The correct direction was used in the landed sub-paragraph (see methodology line 1).

- Reflection prompts answered:

  1. *Instance-verification outcome.* The three-instance framing did not hold. S40 reclassified: its methodology line 1 records D66 strategy-registry framing-vs-as-built drift (the catalogue lists three retrieval strategies, the adapter executes two) — strategy enumeration, not path naming — and none of S40's six methodology lines records an adapter-path-naming drift. The verified count is two: S43's flat-adapter-path drift against the `adapters/outbound/postgres/` convention, and S43b's `tests/contract/http/portfolio/` subdirectory drift against the flat `tests/contract/http/` convention. Promotion still fires: the corrective-discipline-on-first-instance precedent — the metric-threshold and reproducibility-artefacts Patterns-observed entries both promoted at a single instance, because corrective discipline-additions land on a single observed drift event rather than paying further drift instances while waiting for recurrence — covers a two-instance promotion comfortably. The brief anticipated exactly this reclassification at its reconciliation surface 1; the verification discipline worked as designed.

  2. *Sub-paragraph-versus-sibling-entry placement.* The path-naming sub-pattern landed as a sub-paragraph under the existing pre-write reconciliation entry, not as its own sibling Patterns-observed entry. Reasoning: the mitigation surface is identical (pre-write reconciliation reading at session open), the entry already carries a sub-pattern paragraph (Mid-build sub-pattern) so the structural precedent for sub-paragraph altitude is in place, and a sub-class of an already-promoted pattern fits the sub-paragraph altitude. A sibling entry was rejected — it would duplicate the parent entry's mitigation surface and inflate the document's pattern count without structural distinction. A discipline-addition paragraph under the document-tail "Pre-write reconciliation against vendor docs" section was rejected — that section addresses vendor-SDK drift, not codebase-convention drift.

  3. *Captures-as-audit-trail discipline.* All three S43/S43b methodology candidates were captured at `log/captures.md` at the S43b close commit even though only one promotes now; the other two stay pending recurrence with explicit second-instance thresholds. This is the right shape: the captures surface is the audit-trail record of methodology-candidate surfacing, independent of immediate promotion status. Promotion happens at a recurrence threshold or at corrective-discipline-on-first-instance qualification; capture happens at first surfacing. The discipline reinforcement for future session-close work: land every surfaced methodology candidate at captures, not only the ones at promotion threshold, so recurrence tracking has a complete trail.

  4. *Two-versus-three accuracy discipline, and the captures entry as canonical citation source.* The methodology sub-paragraph cites the verified instance count, not the framing-time count carried from the S43b close reflection. The structural-honesty discipline: methodology promotions verify their instance enumeration against the actual session-log methodology lines at every promotion event, and the captures entry's enumeration is the canonical citation source — itself verified against the session log. The captures path-drift entry's append-only body still carries the three-instance framing; its triage resolution line, the designated mutable surface, records the S40 reclassification, so the audit trail shows both the framing-time count and the verified count without falsifying the original capture.

- methodology (line 1): **The pre-write reconciliation discipline caught brief-vs-codebase drift in the very brief drafted to promote it.** The hygiene brief's Entry B template and methodology sub-paragraph parenthetical inverted the S43 path-drift direction — they framed the S43 brief as naming `adapters/outbound/postgres/` against a flat convention, when the S43 session-log entry and the live codebase (`contexts/portfolio/adapters/outbound/postgres/portfolio_repository.py`) both show the reverse: the S43 brief named a flat path, and `adapters/outbound/{vendor}/` is the actual convention. The discipline being promoted caught a drift instance of its own class on the way to being articulated — the cleanest available validation of what the methodology entry claims.

- methodology (line 2): **Prior-conversation summaries reconcile against repo-committed content before becoming brief source material.** The inverted S43 direction traces to cross-conversation brief drafting working from this conversation's loose summaries rather than reconciling against `log/captures.md`, which already carried the correct direction. The discipline addition: when a brief is drafted in a conversation other than the one that produced the source material, the repo-committed artefact — not the prior conversation's summary — is the authority, and the brief drafter reconciles against it before treating the summary as source.

- **Pre-S44 hygiene closed; the brief path-drift sub-pattern is promoted at two verified instances. S44 next.**

S43b is the transport half of the planned substrate-and-transport split begun at S43. It is the first instance of a *planned* bridge session: the two prior bridge instances at P11 (S39 → S39b verification-and-hygiene; S40 → S40b methodologically-clean-artefact authoring) were unplanned corrections discovered at substrate close, and carried `corrects` / `corrected_by` relationships. S43 → S43b carries `paired_with` — S43b completes a planned split, it does not correct S43. No new D-entries: D124 and D125 already commit the architectural surface; the HTTP transport is mechanical absorption from the run_history and optimization router precedents.

- Produced: Five commits closed the session.
  - Commit aba4053 (`docs(p13/s43b)`): the retroactive S43 session-log entry; current-package S43-close plus S43b-in-flight markers; `briefs/p13/s43b.md` preserved.
  - Commit f2a8ec8 (`feat(p13/s43b)`): the HTTP transport surface — two GET routes (`/api/v1/portfolio/cases`, `/cases/{case_id}`), the DTOs, the query parser, the error handlers, and the API composition-root wiring (`AppCompositions.portfolio_reader`, `build_portfolio_reader`, the `PortfolioReaderAdapter`).
  - Commit 00c0f5f (`feat(p13/s43b)`): the HTTP contract tests, the HTTP tenant-isolation scenarios, and the integration lifecycle test.
  - Commit 11c059f (`feat(p13/s43b)`): the image rebuild, the migration verification, the live-stack smoke, and the re-added structural deployment tests.
  - Commit this commit (`docs(p13/s43b)`): this session log entry plus the current-package close marker.

- Decisions: No new D-entries. Per the bridge-session shape — the architectural surface is already committed at D124/D125.

- Tests: 16 new tests at S43b (5 HTTP route tests; 1 integration lifecycle test; 4 HTTP tenant-isolation scenarios; 3 HTTP contract tests; 3 structural deployment-check instances). All S43 tests still green; 28 of 28 import-linter contracts kept; AST enforcement green.

- Pre-write reconciliation: Six surfaces verified before commit 1. Surfaces 1-5 were mechanical — the S43 substrate present and green (28/28); the run_history/optimization router precedents; the AppCompositions plus `_agent_runtime_wiring.py` wiring pattern; the error-handler registration convention; the Dockerfile `COPY contexts ./contexts` picking up `contexts/portfolio/` whole (no per-context `pyproject.toml`, matching the optimization precedent). Surface 6 surfaced one drift — the brief named a `tests/contract/http/portfolio/` subdirectory but the actual S42 convention is flat files; absorbed per the brief's reflection prompt 5 (path drift reconciles against the codebase). Surface 5's migration-deployed structural check resolved at commit 4 (see reflection prompt 3).

- Reflection prompts answered:

  1. *Pre-write reconciliation surfaces.* Surfaces 1-5 mechanical; surface 6 a path drift absorbed per the brief's own discipline. The standout finding sits at surface 5: the migration-deployed-to-tenant-containers structural-honesty check (deferred from S43 commit 9) confirmed the `padhanam-api` image at S43 close predated the `0016` migration file — the S43 `make migrate` (which runs inside that container) applied only through `0015`. The cumulative reconciliation count keeps climbing; the discipline keeps earning its place, and at S43b it caught a genuine deployment-honesty gap rather than only documentation drift.

  2. *Planned-bridge-session sub-variant, first-instance evidence.* The split produced real signal. The S43b transport work — HTTP routes, DTOs, query parser, the API wiring across `_errors.py` / `_agent_runtime_wiring.py` / `main.py`, the three test surfaces, and the live smoke — ran to roughly 1,500 lines across thirteen files. Landing that as commits 8-10 of a single S43 would have been the fatigue-taxed tail of an already-very-long session; the operator's pause at the substrate-completion boundary, and the re-scoping into S43b, meant the transport work got a fresh reconciliation pass, un-rushed wiring, and a live smoke (with its image-rebuild variance) that received full attention rather than being the last push before exhaustion. The planned-bridge sub-variant is distinct from the unplanned S39→S39b / S40→S40b corrections; the `paired_with` metrics relationship captures the distinction structurally. First-instance evidence; second-instance promotion threshold at a future bounded-context substrate session producing the same split shape.

  3. *Image-rebuild structural-honesty check outcome.* The check revealed the image *was* stale at S43 close. `make migrate` runs `python -m ops.migrate` inside the `padhanam-api` container; that container's image predated the `0016` file, so the S43 migrate applied only through `0015`. The S43 contract tests passed regardless because they provision their own synthetic databases and run alembic from the host (which had `0016`) — but the real tenant containers did not carry the portfolio tables. This is exactly the substrate-completion-versus-deployment drift the structural-honesty discipline exists to catch: a migration committed to the source tree is not a migration deployed to the running stack. S43b commit 4 resolved it (rebuild + recreate + migrate) and the structural check is now a durable committed test. The container-image-lag pattern has now recurred across S41, S42, and S43→S43b — well past any promotion threshold for a documented dev-workflow fast-path.

  4. *Operator-pause-at-substrate-close as methodology candidate.* The S43 pause sat at the session-close boundary (substrate complete, transport remaining); the S41 scope check sat at a mid-session boundary (substrate-application). S43b confirms the session-close-boundary variant produced a clean outcome — the planned bridge ran well end to end. The two are sub-variants of one scope-versus-fatigue-management discipline: a scope check is a checkpoint where the remaining work is measured against the current session's fatigue budget, and either fits or earns its own session. Each sub-variant carries its own second-instance promotion threshold.

  5. *HTTP transport mechanical-absorption verification.* Mostly mechanical, with two divergences. The router, DTO, query-parser, and error-handler patterns absorbed cleanly from the run_history and optimization precedents. (a) The test-path drift — brief said `tests/contract/http/portfolio/` subdir, actual convention is flat files — is the *third* instance of the brief-path-drift pattern (S40's adjacent adapter shape; S43's `adapters/outbound/postgres/`; S43b's flat test files), crossing the promotion threshold the S43 entry set. (b) The `PortfolioReaderAdapter` placement diverged from the optimization precedent: the optimization reader adapter lives in `apps/cli/_cross_context.py`, but the portfolio one landed in `apps/api/_agent_runtime_wiring.py` because it is API-only (the portfolio CLI builds `PostgresPortfolioReader` directly) and that module already defines wiring classes locally (`TenantRoutingSourceRepository`) — a defensible placement call, flagged in the commit message.

- methodology (line 1): **Planned-bridge-session sub-variant confirmed at first instance.** A deliberate substrate-and-transport split produced controlled, un-fatigue-taxed transport work; the `paired_with` metrics relationship distinguishes it from the unplanned correction bridges (`corrects`/`corrected_by`). Promotion to `charter/methodology.md` at second instance; see reflection prompt 2.

- methodology (line 2): **Brief path-drift at third instance — crosses the promotion threshold.** Brief drafts naming paths (adapter, test, contract) must reconcile against the actual `adapters/outbound/{vendor}/` and flat-test-file conventions before commit 1. Three instances (S40, S43, S43b) — the S43 entry set the third-instance threshold; this is now a methodology-line promotion candidate for the next hygiene window. See reflection prompt 5.

- methodology (line 3): **Container-image-lag: substrate-completion is not deployment.** A migration committed to the source tree is not a migration on the running stack; `make migrate` runs inside an image that may predate the migration file. The pattern recurred across S41, S42, and S43→S43b; the structural-honesty check at the bridge session is the reliable catch point. Well past promotion threshold for a documented dev-workflow fast-path. See reflection prompt 3.

- **S43b closed; the portfolio context is complete end-to-end through the read surface. S44 next.**

- Tests: 62 new tests (9 shared_kernel, 25 domain, 11 application, 4 CLI, 13 contract); 28 of 28 import-linter contracts kept; AST enforcement green.

- Pre-write reconciliation: The S43 six-surface reconciliation surfaced one mechanical placement (schema.md section ordering), four code-state drifts from the P12 audit-finding snapshots absorbed into the schema.md content (TenantContext.tenant_id and ErrorResponse.correlation_id typed `str` not `UUID`; ErrorResponse a flat `BaseModel`; the cursor-codec sites a three-singular / one-plural split with the audit codec originating at S36), a repository-adapter path drift (`adapters/outbound/postgres/`, not the brief's flat path), the protocol-location question (resolved to `shared_kernel/` in prose ahead of commit 1), and the import-linter delta (27 → 28). Three further adjustments surfaced at execution time, each flagged in its commit message: commits 2 and 3 swapped because the domain entities import the `shared_kernel` types the brief's commit 3 created (dependency graph); `jurisdiction` was restored to all three portfolio tables and entities because the brief's field lists omitted it and D12 binds it as a column from inception; the ports landed with the adapter commit rather than the use-case commit because the adapter structurally implements them.

- Reflection prompts answered:

  1. *Pre-write reconciliation surfaces.* The pattern across S43 reconciliation: the brief was accurate on architecture and intent, drifted on paths and field-completeness. The four audit-finding-snapshot drifts were mechanical absorptions into the binding spec; the three execution-time adjustments (commits 2/3 swap, jurisdiction restoration, ports-with-adapter) were dependency-graph and charter-compliance corrections, not architectural disagreements. None required a course change — the substrate landed as designed.

  2. *Forward-compat substrate-depth discipline, first build instance.* Case/DataPoint/Assertion landed in full; the Revisable Protocol shape committed without being exercised across the full Phase 2-A substrate (only DataPoint implements it at S43). Two forward-compat affordances landed unused: `DataPoint.certainty` (nullable, reserved for the D117 tiered-by-salience implementation at P15) and the ActorReference placeholder (superseded at S44 by ActorContext). Neither felt premature — both are single-column / single-field additions now versus refactor-forcing retrofits later, the D12 column-from-inception reasoning generalised. First-instance evidence that the forward-compat substrate-depth discipline holds at build time, not only at framing time.

  3. *Charter-touchpoint discipline at session-1 of a new package.* Commit 1 front-loaded D124, D125, the schema.md section, the epic-note line, the current-package transition, and the brief preservation before any code. Heavier than a mid-package session — and it served the audit-trail discipline rather than burdening it: the binding specification (schema.md table shapes, the D-entry alternatives analysis) landed before the code, so every subsequent commit reconciled against a committed spec rather than the spec chasing the code.

  4. *CLI-as-smoke-write-path discipline.* The `padhanam portfolio` CLI landed ahead of any HTTP write surface specifically to give the (S43b) live-stack smoke an honest write path — without it the smoke would seed Cases via raw SQL, bypassing the audit-events port. The CLI's command surface drove a substrate change: the `create-data-point` action required a fifth use case (`create_data_point`) the brief's four-use-case list had not named. Methodology candidate: substrate sessions that ship a read-side HTTP surface without the matching write side should plan a CLI write path by default, and the CLI command surface sanity-checks the use-case set before the use cases are considered complete.

  5. *Operator-pause-at-substrate-completion as methodology candidate.* S43 closed at 7 of 10 commits at operator decision, after a Claude Code check-in surfaced the remaining three commits' scope. The pause sat at the substrate-completion boundary — domain, application, adapter, and CLI layers complete and green; the HTTP transport surface and the live-stack smoke remaining. Two forces drove it: the ~600 lines of intricate, interdependent API-wiring code still to write (routes, DTOs, query parser, the `_cross_context.py` wiring adapter, the `_agent_runtime_wiring.py` factory, `AppCompositions`, error handlers), and commit 9's image-rebuild step carrying real environmental variance. The operator re-scoped the remainder as S43b, a planned bridge session. This is the first instance of a deliberate substrate-and-transport split: the two prior bridge instances at P11 (S39 → S39b verification-and-hygiene; S40 → S40b methodologically-clean-artefact authoring) were unplanned corrections discovered at substrate close, and carried `corrects` / `corrected_by` relationships. S43 → S43b carries `paired_with` instead — S43b completes a planned split, it does not correct S43. The planned-bridge sub-variant is the load-bearing methodology data point of this session; first-instance evidence is captured here, and promotion to a `charter/methodology.md` line waits on a second instance of a future bounded-context substrate session producing the same split shape. The session-close-boundary pause also pairs with the S41 mid-session scope check at the substrate-application boundary: two sub-variants — mid-session and session-close — of one scope-versus-fatigue-management discipline, each with its own second-instance promotion threshold.

  6. *Methodology line opportunities.* Four candidates surfaced. The protocol-file-location convention settled at `shared_kernel/` — a cross-context Protocol referencing no context type belongs there, not at `padhanam/protocols/`, because the `platform-no-contexts` import-linter contract plus the shared-kernel-as-cross-context-type-home charter both point there. The path-drift pattern reached its second instance (S40's adjacent retrieval_evaluation adapter shape; S43's `adapters/outbound/postgres/` versus the brief's flat path) — brief drafts naming adapter and test paths must reconcile against the actual `adapters/outbound/{vendor}/` and flat-test-file conventions before commit 1; a third instance promotes it. The Revisable Protocol is the first cross-context Protocol in the codebase — the generic-Protocol-imports-no-context-type discipline is a first-instance observation. Case-as-substrate-vocabulary: the karma portfolio-of-relationships domain language landed as the context's first-class vocabulary (`portfolio` over `state_persistence`).

- methodology (line 1): **Planned-bridge-session sub-variant.** A deliberate substrate-and-transport split, distinct from the unplanned verification-and-hygiene (S39→S39b) and methodologically-clean-artefact (S40→S40b) sub-variants; first instance at S43→S43b, carrying `paired_with` rather than `corrects`. ## 2026-05-21 — Charter-export tooling commit (parallel-block close before S43)
roles: engineer, technical writer, analyst
mode: build (tooling commit; closes parallel-block work surfaced at pre-P13 hygiene close)

- Produced: One substantive commit plus this session log entry.
  - Commit c3f5040 (`feat(scripts/charter-export)`): allowlist-based charter snapshot tooling at `scripts/charter-export.py` reading `docs/charter-archive-manifest.md` as single source of truth; `Makefile` `charter-export` target; manifest pending-confirmation items resolved per operator positions (`p13-epic.md` kept; `framing-landing-commit.md` kept; `s43.md` added with deferred drop at S43 close) and the "Pending operator confirmation" section removed; manifest file-count tally corrected to 48. Dry-run and live-run verified at 48 files; live-run produced `charter-20260521-1403/` plus `charter-20260521-1403.zip`, both git-ignored.
  - Commit this commit (`docs(p13)`): this session log entry.

- Decisions: No new D-entries. Tooling commit; no architectural commitment.

- Tests: None. Tooling commit; dry-run plus live-run verify the script behaves; no test-suite extension.

- Pre-write reconciliation: Five surfaces verified before commit 1. Surface 1 found `scripts/` is a pre-existing directory (seven P9-P11 live-stack smoke scripts), not new as the brief framed it — commit-body paragraph 3 and reflection prompt 3 were rewritten to the honest framing on operator disposition. Surface 2's dry-run reported 47 files (48 after the `s43.md` allowlist addition), not the brief's 45/46 — the manifest tally and the brief both carried an arithmetic error (the component breakdown summed to 47); manifest tally corrected to 48 and `.gitignore` confirmed `/*.zip` is the existing repo-root pattern. Surface 3 found `.gitignore` already covers all three output patterns — no `.gitignore` edit; the commit landed three files, not four. Surfaces 4 (manifest pending-items) and 5 (no scope creep) were as expected. Two operator dispositions resolved in prose ahead of commit 1.

- Reflection prompts answered:

  1. *Parallel-block-converges as session shape.* This session's work shape — parallel work the operator builds incrementally outside the session sequence, converging into a single commit when ready — is structurally distinct from the four session shapes named in `charter/methodology.md` (substrate, bridge, transport, hygiene). It is not substrate (no bounded context or domain layer), not bridge (no verification-and-hygiene gap between two build sessions), not transport (no HTTP surface), and not cleanly hygiene (hygiene closes accumulated carryover or drift; this closes a discrete capability built in parallel). The closest precedent is the brand-guidelines-plus-`tokens.css` block discussed at the S30b conversation: parallel work converging into a commit when ready. This is the first instance to land with a session-log entry that names the shape explicitly. Whether it earns a fifth shape name (parallel-block-close) or absorbs under hygiene at the lighter end is a second-instance question; the distinguishing property worth tracking is the convergence pattern — artefacts accumulate in the working tree outside the session sequence, then land as a micro-block when the operator judges them ready, rather than being scoped into a brief up front. Recurrence test fires at the next parallel-block convergence; promotion threshold at second instance.

  2. *Manifest pending-items as commit-time discipline.* The manifest was drafted with three open items parked in a "Pending operator confirmation" section at its tail rather than blocking the draft until every allowlist decision was settled. The pattern carries audit-trail value: the draft captured what was genuinely uncertain at draft time (two files added beyond the original spec, one held out), the operator's resolution landed at commit time, and the draft-uncertainty → resolution trail is legible. The alternatives are worse — blocking the draft slows the parallel-block work, and silently folding the uncertain items in hides the judgement call. The discipline's shape: park genuinely-open questions visibly in the artefact, resolve at commit time, then migrate the resolved audit trail into the commit message and session log so the artefact itself does not carry permanent low-value pending-state content (the "Pending operator confirmation" section was deleted at this commit for exactly that reason). First-instance observation; recurrence test fires at the next document drafted with open questions at its tail.

  3. *scripts/ widens from smoke-only to smoke-plus-repo-discipline.* The directory has carried live-stack smoke-verification scripts (`smoke_p9_s32.py` through `smoke_p11_s42.py`) since P9. `charter-export.py` is the first non-smoke entry, extending the directory's scope to repo-discipline tooling. The two-altitude convention itself (`ops/` as importable runtime package; `scripts/` as standalone-executable directory) is pre-existing and stable; this commit widens what `scripts/` carries, not what altitudes exist. The widening is mechanically clean (standalone executable, no package import contract change), so the directory's structural contract held without modification. Methodology observation candidate: the two-altitude convention deserves documentation at a future hygiene window if a third widening or third altitude surfaces; without documentation, the next tooling addition risks landing under the wrong altitude. Not promoted to a binding commitment at this session; recurrence test fires at the next tooling addition.

  4. *Brief-authoring-time verification gap — triple-instance evidence.* The pre-write reconciliation discipline has now caught a brief-asserted factual error about repo state in each of the last three sessions. P13 framing landing: the brief asserted the D116/D117/D118 architecture-primitive numbers without verifying against current `charter/decisions.md` and `charter/architecture.md`; reconciliation caught the D-number scramble. Pre-P13 hygiene: the brief asserted four field shapes from P12 audit-finding snapshots without verifying against current code; reconciliation caught four divergences. This session: the brief asserted `scripts/` as a new directory and inherited a 45-file tally without verifying against repo state or the breakdown's own arithmetic; reconciliation caught both. The pattern generalises from D-numbers to any factual claim about repo state. The P13 framing landing methodology line set a recurrence test at P14 framing; this session fires that test early and in a broader shape — the gap is not D-reference-specific. The structural reading: strategic-mode brief-authoring either verifies factual claims about repo state against available sources (project knowledge for charter content; explicit pre-write-reconciliation marking for working-tree state a Claude.ai conversation cannot reach), or carries the assertion as a known-to-need-verification marker rather than as a bare fact. The current bare-fact pattern produces structural-honesty findings reliably — the downstream reconciliation works — but it pushes verification cost to the commit session, where reversal is more expensive (a false claim in a drafted brief becomes a false claim in a commit body unless caught). Charter methodology promotion candidate at the next hygiene window; this is the strongest of the three instances, because it is the third and because it shows the gap is general rather than D-reference-specific.

- methodology (line 1): **Parallel-block-close as a candidate fifth session shape.** Parallel work accumulated in the working tree outside the session sequence, landed as a micro-block when ready — distinct from substrate, bridge, transport, and hygiene. First instance named in a session log; see reflection prompt 1. Promotion threshold at second instance.

- methodology (line 2): **The brief-authoring-time verification gap is triple-instance evidenced and general.** Strategic-mode briefs assert factual claims about repo state without verification; pre-write reconciliation has caught one such error per session across the last three sessions, spanning D-numbers, code field-shapes, and directory/arithmetic state. Charter methodology promotion candidate at the next hygiene window; see reflection prompt 4.

- **Charter-export tooling committed; working tree clean entering S43.**

The third-instance temporal-position taxonomy (pre-audit, post-audit, pre-next-phase hygiene) does not absorb this instance: all three of those positions sit at phase boundaries, while this hygiene session sits intra-package — after P13 framing closed but before S43, the package's first build session, opens. It warrants a fourth sub-position, pre-package-first-session hygiene. A distinguishing structural property reinforces the new sub-position: this is the first hygiene session whose work surface was set by an explicit framing decision (P13 framing Decision 2 bundled the two items) rather than by carryover triaged at hygiene-open time. First-instance evidence for the pre-package-first-session sub-shape; recurrence test fires at the next package framing that bundles hygiene into a pre-first-session session.

  2. *Pre-write reconciliation, twelfth-plus instance.* The five surfaces split three mechanical (schema.md structure, phase-2-audit-inputs.md state, current-package.md close marker — each file in its assumed state) and one structural-honesty (surface 2's code-substrate verification) plus one that drove commit-shape (surface 3's grep). Surface 2 surfaced four points of drift between the P12 audit-finding snapshots and current code state — every field-table type and structural claim in the brief's schema.md content needed at least one correction, and the cursor-codec sites table had both a wrong path and a wrong originating session. The discipline earned its place decisively here: surface 2 alone caught four documentation errors that would otherwise have shipped into a binding-specification surface and propagated forward as ground truth. Surface 3's grep returned non-zero (four non-allowlist files), so commit 2 stayed substantive rather than compressing to the brief's contingency no-op. The twelfth-plus instance confirms the pattern is past any promotion threshold; the open question is no longer whether to keep it but whether to push verification one step upstream (the framing-landing session's methodology line already proposed a brief-authoring-time D-number checkpoint; the same logic applies to code-substrate snapshots — a brief that cites code field-shapes should verify them against current code at brief-authoring time).

  3. *Grep allowlist as cross-context discipline.* The S13 verification-grep allowlist began as a rebrand-sub-shape playbook element — a literal three-path list (`charter/decisions.md`, `log/sessions.md`, `docs/archive/`). This session generalises it on three axes, each a first-instance observation with a recurrence test at the next narrative-cleanup operation. (a) The allowlist is not a closed enumeration but an expression of an append-only-protected *category* defined by a structural property: any file that records what was true at a specific past moment, regardless of directory. Dated point-in-time snapshots (`docs/notes/status-*.md`) qualify by construction; so do verbatim-by-mandate build-session briefs under `briefs/` — surfaced at commit 4 when preserving this very prompt, whose title carries the old platform name. Rephrasing such a file would falsify its record, a worse violation of append-only spirit than the literal grep-rule violation. (b) The rebrand-cleanup discipline operates at the principle level — old-platform-name scrubbing from current-facing narrative — not the literal-string level; this is why the verification grep widened from `Quorum` to `(Quorum|Zephyr)`, since Zephyr is structurally equivalent (an old platform name, archived by D38, legitimate in historical content only). One operation, not two. (c) Use-mention occurrences require structural-descriptor rephrasing, not direct find-replace: `p13-epic.md` named this hygiene task itself, where a literal replace produces nonsense and allowlist-extension creates a permanent stale-name carrier. The S13 rebrand playbook did not anticipate use-mention because S13 was the rebrand event, not a post-rebrand cleanup; this session is the first post-rebrand cleanup operation.

  4. *Phase 2-A close hygiene list as new convention surface.* Commit 3 established the "Phase 2-A close hygiene list" section in `charter/phase-2-audit-inputs.md` as the accumulator for closure-housekeeping items triaged to the Phase 2-A close per P13 framing Decision 2. It is structurally distinct from two adjacent surfaces: the substrate-completion list and `charter/deferred-decisions.md` both carry activation-gated deferred work (substrate awaiting explicit trigger conditions), whereas this list carries closure-housekeeping with no activation gate — items clear at the Phase 2-A close hygiene session regardless of trigger. The roadmap stale-headers refresh lands as the first entry. First-instance evidence for the convention; recurrence test fires at the first item added through Phase 2-A package execution beyond this opening entry. One placement observation: the section co-locates with four audit-input sections in a file oriented to the Phase 2 close audit; close-hygiene and audit-input are different categories, and the co-location holds per the brief's explicit instruction (both are Phase 2 forward-accumulators) but is the kind of surface a future hygiene session might split if the list grows.

- methodology (line 1): **Pre-package-first-session hygiene is a fourth temporal sub-position for the hygiene-session shape.** The prior three-position taxonomy (pre-audit, post-audit, pre-next-phase) is phase-boundary-scoped; this instance sits intra-package between package framing close and the first build session, with its work surface set by an explicit framing decision rather than accumulated carryover. First-instance evidence; see reflection prompt 1.

- methodology (line 2): **The narrative-cleanup verification-grep allowlist is an append-only-protected category defined by structural property, not a literal path list.** Any file that records what was true at a past moment — `docs/archive/`, dated `docs/notes/status-*.md` snapshots, verbatim-preserved `briefs/` prompts — qualifies; rephrasing one would falsify its record. First-instance generalisation; see reflection prompt 3(a).

- methodology (line 3): **Rebrand-cleanup operates at the principle level — old-platform-name scrubbing — not the literal-string level.** The verification grep widened to every former platform name (`Quorum` and `Zephyr`) because the workitem's principle is drift removal, not a single search term. First-instance evidence; see reflection prompt 3(b).

- methodology (line 4): **Use-mention occurrences in rebrand-cleanup require structural-descriptor rephrasing, not direct find-replace.** Where text names the cleanup task or the rename event rather than referring to the platform by its old name, a literal replace produces nonsense; the generic descriptor preserves clarity without carrying the literal. First post-rebrand-cleanup instance; see reflection prompt 3(c).

- methodology (line 5): **The post-session verification grep caught drift the session introduced in its own close marker.** Commit 4's close marker named the two former platform names as a use-mention; the post-commit-5 `(Quorum|Zephyr)` grep flagged `charter/current-package.md`, corrected at commit 6. The verification step is not redundant with careful authoring — it caught an own-goal authoring missed. A narrative-cleanup session should run its verification grep after every commit, including the commits that describe the cleanup.

- **Pre-P13 hygiene closed; S43 next per `briefs/p13/s43.md`.**

## 2026-05-20 — Post-P12 methodology comparison strategic block
roles: analyst, PM, technical writer
mode: strategic (charter commits only; the deck.html build commit deferred to Phase 2 close per operator decision)

- Produced: Two commits closed the session.
  - Commit 1 (`docs(charter)`, dc74ea7): three entries appended to the "Methodology and governance enhancements" sub-section of `charter/deferred-decisions.md` (maintenance and continuous-operation discipline; multi-implementer extension hypothesis; methodology comparison act for the case-study deck); the drafted comparison content at the new `charter/methodology-comparison.md`; the strategic-block close marker at `charter/current-package.md`.
  - Commit 2 (this commit, `docs(log)`): this session log entry.

- Decisions: No new D-entries. Three deferred-decisions entries committed with differential postures — the maintenance entry as a forward commitment (load-bearing methodology workitem, initial articulation at Phase 1 close, drawing on D78 personal-use evidence), the multi-implementer entry as a forward hypothesis (no specific phase-boundary commitment, hypothesise-test-iterate when evidence at non-solo altitudes accumulates), and the comparison-act entry as a Phase 2-close deferral. The maintenance and multi-implementer entries draw on D78 and the bet's solo-capacity framing as substrate.

- Tests: n/a. Charter documents only; no code, no enforcement surface touched.

- Scope revision: The opening brief specified a methodology comparison act inserted into `charter/deck.html` plus an act renumber. Mid-session the operator decided the comparison content — which makes procurement-facing factual claims about six named third-party methodologies — must pass human verification, and that verification and deck integration happen together at Phase 2 close. The deliverable was re-scoped: the comparison content landed as a draft at `charter/methodology-comparison.md`, the deferral was logged as a third deferred-decisions entry, and `charter/deck.html` was not modified.

- Reflection prompts answered:

  1. *Pre-write reconciliation outcome.* The six reconciliation items surfaced four pieces of drift. The brief's named token source `brand-tokens.css` is not linked into `deck.html` and is a separate dark-mode application design system; the deck is self-contained with its own inline `:root`. `log/sessions.md` is newest-at-top, not append-at-bottom as the brief stated. The brief referenced a comparison table "per the strategic conversation that produced this prompt" but did not transcribe it, and it was not preserved anywhere in the repo. A nine-column table with paragraph-length cells would not render cleanly in the deck's 720px prose column. The deferred-decisions insertion point and the current-package close-marker placement both resolved cleanly. The missing-table-content finding is what triggered the scope revision.

  2. *Methodology comparison as Phase 1 close substrate.* The comparison did not land as a deck act, so it creates no immediate reading-pressure on the Phase 1 close audit. The maintenance-discipline entry still creates that pressure independently: it commits the methodology document's first prescriptive articulation of continuous-operation discipline to the Phase 1 close authoring session. The multi-implementer hypothesis sits correctly as a deferred-decisions entry with a much-later activation horizon and consumes no methodology-document oxygen at Phase 1 close.

  3. *Differential cell treatment as discipline.* The Padhanam row's four postures — truly-TBD on reverse engineering, phase-deferred on throughput, forward commitment on maintenance, forward hypothesis on multi-implementer — are preserved in the `methodology-comparison.md` draft. The honest-accounting discipline held even as the artefact itself was deferred: the draft is explicitly marked unverified, the third-party claims carry accuracy flags, and the deferral is logged rather than the artefact rushed to publication.

  4. *Methodology-document promotion implications.* No promotion candidate at this session. The brief's proposed candidate (comparative-artefact discipline plus posture-differentiation) is first-instance and does not meet the captures-candidate threshold of structural novelty or threefold recurrence. The session does surface one genuinely novel observation, recorded as a methodology line below.

- methodology (line 1): **Pre-write reconciliation can surface a readiness gap in the deliverable itself, not only drift in facts.** The brief specified a deck act and referenced a comparison table it did not contain. Pre-write reconciliation surfaced the absence; the disciplined response was to re-scope the deliverable (draft now, verify and publish later) rather than fabricate content to fill the gap. First instance of reconciliation catching a deliverable-readiness gap as distinct from a fact-drift gap; recurrence test at future briefs that reference un-transcribed substrate.

- methodology (line 2): **First load-bearing application of the proper-noun-attribution discipline.** The "Methodology mechanical-enforcement upgrades" deferred entry names a proper-noun-attribution check on model-drafted vendor-voice artefacts. This session is the first time that discipline blocked a publication: the comparison table's claims about six named third-party methodologies were judged not verification-ready for a public artefact, and the operator deferred publication. The discipline anticipated exactly this class of content. Worth noting at the Phase 1 close audit as evidence the deferred-decisions backlog item is correctly specified.

- **Post-P12 methodology comparison strategic block closed** at 2026-05-20. The next strategic-mode block is Phase 2 design 7-Step arc Step 6 Pass 3, continuing separately.

The specific workitem framing is also now sharper: the Disaggregator role would benefit from a temporal-lifecycle discipline addition plus orthogonal-dimension handling; the candidate landing surfaces (system_prompt addition versus Phase 2 skills-per-role surface) are concrete enough for Phase 2 strategic-mode opening to settle. The workitem moves from hypothetical (something the methodology authoring might need) to scoped (what specifically and where).

- Reflection prompt 3: **Methodology-fit lifecycle sub-branch cross-branch implications.** Sub-branch 6.4 surfaces dependencies on Branches 1 (portfolio composition shifts at key-change moments), 4 (mirror format differs in rhythm versus key-change states), and 5 (trust required to ask "is this a key-change moment"). The question: should these cross-branch dependencies land as explicit cross-references in the issue tree at a future Step's refinement, or stay implicit until Step 3's prioritisation surfaces them. Verdict: leave implicit at Step 2; surface during Step 3 prioritisation reasoning. Two reasons. First, MECE discipline at the sub-branch level holds without cross-references: each sub-branch carries distinct work units, and the temporal-lifecycle cross-cutting discipline already names the lifecycle-stage behaviour applies at every branch; adding explicit dependency edges would make the output graph-shaped rather than tree-shaped, which exceeds the McKinsey override's "issue tree" framing. Second, dependency ordering is one of the four Step 2 open questions explicitly named for Step 3; surfacing dependencies now would pre-empt Step 3 work. Step 4 (workplan) is where explicit dependency artefacts will likely live if the prioritisation produces them; the issue tree stays tree-shaped at Step 2.

- Reflection prompt 4: **Synthetic-retrospective-brief pattern.** First instance at this session. The pattern emerged because the operator's Claude.ai usage continued from Step 1 close into Step 2 work within the same conversation, so no separate Step 2 opening prompt existed. The retrospective brief documents intent, inputs, and discipline that would have lived in a pre-conversation brief. Recurrence test at Step 3: if Step 3 opens with a formal pre-conversation brief at Claude.ai (the target shape; pre-conversation brief drafts before substantive Step 3 work begins), the synthetic-retrospective pattern stays at single instance. If Step 3 also transitions mid-conversation, the pattern lifts to second instance and earns methodology-candidate promotion. Worth surfacing now as discipline: the same-conversation-transition shape is partly a function of the operator's Claude.ai usage pattern (whether each Step gets a fresh conversation versus continuing from prior Step's close). The discipline-correction is to draft Step 3's pre-conversation brief intentionally before Claude.ai opens for Step 3, regardless of whether the conversation continues from Step 2. The brief at `briefs/phase-2/design-7step-step-3.md` lands as the test of whether the briefs/ discipline survives operator vigilance now that it has been re-established at Step 1.

- methodology (line 1): **Replace-versus-append ambiguity in commit-prompt wording when the underlying file has append-only-at-section-level discipline.** The Step 2 commit prompt said "Replace the 'Step 1 closed; Step 2 next' framing with 'Step 2 closed; Step 3 next.'" The file's pattern across six prior close markers is append-not-replace per the methodology document's section-level append-only discipline. The structurally honest interpretation is append, with the latest paragraph serving as the source of truth and the audit trail preserved by the prior paragraphs standing. Future commit prompts editing current-package.md or any append-only-at-section-level file should use "append a new close marker after the prior one" wording rather than "replace the framing" to avoid the ambiguity. First explicit instance; recurrence test at Step 3 commit prompt drafting (does the operator's commit-prompt convention shift to append-shaped wording, or does the ambiguity recur).

- methodology (line 2): **Synthetic-retrospective-brief pattern.** First instance at this session. Pattern emerges when a strategic-mode block transitions mid-conversation (from one Step to the next within the same Claude.ai conversation) and no separate opening prompt exists for the second block. The retrospective brief documents intent/inputs/discipline at the commit session rather than before the conversation. Honest framing requires the brief file to flag the synthetic-retrospective shape explicitly (done at the top of `briefs/phase-2/design-7step-step-2.md`). Recurrence test at Step 3: pre-conversation brief at `briefs/phase-2/design-7step-step-3.md` before Claude.ai conversation opens for Step 3 keeps the pattern at single instance and validates the discipline-correction; same-conversation transition lifts the pattern to second instance and promotes the candidate accordingly. Discipline-correction is forward-looking: draft Step 3's pre-conversation brief intentionally before opening Claude.ai for Step 3, regardless of whether the conversation continues from Step 2.

- methodology (line 3): **Methodology-template extensibility-without-breaking as procurement-grade evidence signal across two roles.** The Disaggregator role at Step 2 and the ProblemFramer role at Step 1 both have narrower authored discipline scope than the work each Step's conversation needed. In both cases the role's structural discipline accommodated operator-driven extensions without breaking. Pattern now consistent across two roles. The methodology-extension workitem (expand role system_prompts versus skills-per-role surface deferred per briefs/p8/mckinsey-7-step.md) moves from single-instance hypothetical to two-instance pattern-based candidate for Phase 2 framing. Recurrence test continues at Steps 3 through 7 (Prioritiser, Workplanner, Analyst, Synthesiser, Storyteller roles); if all seven McKinsey 7-Step roles surface the same pattern, the workitem lifts to high-priority Phase 2 framing input with five-to-seven-instance recurrence evidence well past any structural-promotion threshold.

- **Phase 2 design 7-Step arc Step 2 commit closed.** The next strategic-mode block is Phase 2 design 7-Step arc Step 3 (Prioritisation), opening at Claude.ai. Step 3 takes the thirty sub-problems plus the four open questions and produces the impact-tractability prioritisation, ranking sub-problems and surfacing the prioritised set as Step 4 (workplan) input plus the bets that land as Phase 2 LVT structure at the v6 roadmap entry per D44. The Step 3 pre-conversation brief should land at `briefs/phase-2/design-7step-step-3.md` before the Step 3 Claude.ai conversation opens, restoring full briefs/ discipline and testing the synthetic-retrospective-brief pattern's single-instance verdict.

The first is the structurally honest shape because the commit-session prompt is itself preservation-bearing per D43; preserving a template-with-placeholders is preserving incomplete content. Recurrence test continues at the next Claude.ai-conversation-to-Claude-Code commit-session transition (likely Step 2's commit session).

- Reflection prompt 2: **Methodology refinements coherence verdict.** The three refinements integrated cleanly with v2 super-section grouping. Foundation super-section landed as a natural grouping for content that was already conceptually grouped but not structurally; the new Hypothesis and iteration sub-section reads as a natural extension of "What's being investigated" rather than as a substantial reframing — the iteration cycle was implicit throughout the v2 document and v3 makes it explicit. Work organisation super-section restructure was the most substantive change; the six-sub-section structure (Strategic tree, Phase lifecycle, Package lifecycle, Session lifecycle, Session shapes, Frameworks) makes lifecycle disciplines visible without bloating the document because the new sub-sections (Phase lifecycle, Package lifecycle, Session lifecycle) replace prose that was previously distributed across multiple H2s. Measurement model hypothesis-framing is a one-paragraph addition that operates as a meta-commitment about the model itself; the framing was implicit at v2 and v3 makes it explicit. The methodology document and architecture.md are properly delimited: methodology covers how Padhanam itself is built (operator discipline, build process, framing-prompt patterns, refactoring conventions, session shapes, measurement model); architecture covers what Padhanam is built as (architectural commitments, patterns, contexts, vendor posture, domain primitives, substrate scaffolding). No overlap; the Cross-document map in architecture.md and the Charter structure sub-section in methodology.md establish the boundary explicitly. Future hygiene at Phase 2 audit may surface additional refinement candidates if Phase 2 evidence demands; the v3 hypothesis is that Foundation + Work organisation + Measurement model refinements absorb the post-P12 evidence and the document is sustainable through Phase 2 without further structural change.

- Reflection prompt 3: **Three-document relationship operationalisation.** The three-document relationship holds up structurally at this session's close. The principles.md header pointer (architecture.md for synthesis; decisions.md for full reasoning; methodology.md for build discipline) establishes the navigation convention at the entry surface readers reach first. The methodology document's Charter structure sub-section names architecture.md as a fourth Strategic constant alongside bet.md, principles.md, methodology.md; the strategic-constants framing makes the four-document relationship visible. The architecture.md Cross-document map section names principles + decisions + architecture as the three reading-mode triad (session-open compact; cold-path audit-time; warm-path synthesis-time) and names methodology + product-methodology as adjacent surfaces (build methodology; product methodology layer for users). Together the three cross-references describe a coherent reading order: at session open, read principles.md (compact rules); for full reasoning, read decisions.md (active phase) or the per-phase archive; for synthesis, read architecture.md (with diagrams); for build discipline, read methodology.md; for product methodology, read product-methodology.md. Each document has a distinct audience and a distinct reading moment. Phase 2 framing benefits from reading architecture.md alongside the bet, the PRFAQ, principles.md, decisions.md (active), and the consolidated Phase 2 inputs file: architecture.md gives the procurement-grade-touch synthesis surface that Phase 2 framing's strategic-mode block reads as the structural anchor for "what we have built and how it relates to the proposition". Forward-relevance for Phase 2 close: architecture.md v2 absorbs Phase 2 substrate additions; methodology.md v4 absorbs Phase 2 discipline-pattern evidence; principles.md gains new principles as Phase 2 surfaces commitments worth promoting to binding rules; decisions.md (Phase 2 active) accumulates Phase 2 D-entries until Phase 2 close, at which point Phase 2 D-entries archive to `docs/archive/decisions/phase-2.md` per the per-phase archival pattern.

- methodology (line 1): **Pre-Phase-2 strategic-mode session is a third hygiene-shape instance.** First instance was pre-P12 hygiene at P11 close (six commits, structural-finding focus). Second instance was post-P12 charter-discipline hygiene at the post-audit boundary (seven commits, discipline-convention focus). Third instance is this session (ten commits, synthesis-and-refinement focus). The accumulation of three hygiene-shape sessions at three different temporal positions (mid-P11-close, post-audit, pre-Phase-2-framing) reinforces the methodology document v2's framing that hygiene sessions plan by default at phase boundaries. The three instances also surface a sub-shape distinction worth naming for future audit observation: pre-audit hygiene (P11 close) addresses substrate-session debt; post-audit hygiene (post-P12) addresses charter-discipline debt the audit surfaced but explicitly scoped out; pre-next-phase hygiene (this session) addresses synthesis-and-cleaned-state-for-framing needs that the audit-output state doesn't fully satisfy. The three sub-shapes may consolidate or stay distinct at Phase 2 close audit observation.

- methodology (line 2): **Architecture.md as new charter surface absorbs synthesis-density load that principles.md cannot carry without losing scannability and decisions.md cannot carry without losing per-D-entry independence.** The three-document relationship resolves a tension implicit at Phase 1 that surfaced explicitly at P12 close: principles.md is read every session and must stay tight; decisions.md carries 113 D-entries each with Choice/Reasoning/Alternatives/Kano and is structurally optimised for per-entry audit-trail not for cross-entry synthesis. Procurement-grade-touch reading requires synthesis with diagrams that neither surface naturally accommodates. Architecture.md as a third synthesis surface fills the gap. The Cross-document map in architecture.md plus the principles header pointer plus the methodology Charter structure update operationalise the relationship across all three surfaces; the convention scales to v2 of architecture.md at Phase 2 close. Phase 2 close audit reviews the relationship's coherence under Phase 2 evidence (does architecture.md still synthesise cleanly when Phase 2 adds UX layer concepts and workflow context substrate? does the principles header pointer remain accurate? does the methodology Charter structure list need expansion?).

- methodology (line 3): **Methodology document refinements absorbed structurally without v3 surfacing tensions.** The v3 hypothesis is that Foundation hypothesis-iteration, Work organisation lifecycle expansion, and Measurement model hypothesis-framing make the document's hypothesis-iteration nature visible at the structural level rather than only at the content level. v1 articulated the discipline; v2 absorbed Phase 1 evidence into Patterns-observed entries; v3 surfaces the iteration mechanic explicitly so that Phase 2's evidence has a structural place to land. The hypothesis-as-living-cycle framing in Hypothesis and iteration sub-section makes the methodology's revision posture explicit ("the methodology document is a living hypothesis revised across phase boundaries"); the Work organisation lifecycle structure makes phase-and-package-and-session boundaries visible at the document's level rather than only at the project's level; the Measurement model hypothesis-framing makes the metric model itself a hypothesis. Forward-relevance: Phase 2 close audit reviews whether v3's structural reframing actually absorbs Phase 2's evidence without further structural change, or whether v4 needs additional super-sections (e.g., a dedicated cross-cutting Patterns super-section grouping Patterns-observed plus Failure-modes-observed plus What-Padhanam-does-not-do plus the principle sub-sections at the document's tail).

- **Pre-Phase-2 architecture synthesis and methodology refinement closed.** The next strategic-mode block is Phase 2 packaging. Phase 2 framing reads the four-document charter (bet, principles with three-document pointer at top, architecture, methodology v3) plus the consolidated `charter/p12-phase-2-inputs.md` handoff section (which now includes the Updates since P12 close section listing all post-P12 work). Phase 2 framing produces a v6 roadmap entry per D44, a Phase 2 PRD per D43, and the first Phase 2 package's epic note per D43.

- Findings outcomes captured per the brief's pre-write reconciliation framing:

No operator engagement on placement; the cluster-choice was clear at inspection.
